Court leans toward narrow ruling on inmate’s use of habeas for accelerated transfer to a halfway house or home confinement

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

Type to search articles, cases, and authors.

The Supreme Court heard oral argument on Nov. 2 in Maxwell v. Dinis about whether a federal inmate may use a habeas statute, 28 U.S.C. § 2241, to contest the Federal Bureau of Prisons’ refusal to accelerate his transfer to a halfway house or home confinement.

Maxwell, a former attorney, was convicted at trial for his role in conspiring with a mob-related organization, La Costa Nostra, to take over a Texas-based mortgage-loan company, FirstPlus Financial Group, Inc., and drain it of its assets. As Maxwell neared the end of his sentence, he sought to contest the BOP’s calculation of good-time credits that would accelerate his transfer from prison to a halfway house or home confinement. The question for the court was whether Section 2241, which does not clearly spell out what claims may be brought but whose scope has largely been shaped by precedent, allows him to do so.

Maxwell’s attorney, Masha Hansford, pressed for a broad rule that habeas is available to an inmate like Maxwell who is asserting a right to a “distinct and lower level of custody.” But that position almost immediately encountered some pushback. Justice Ketanji Brown Jackson questioned “whether we really need to use this case to define every circumstance in which habeas is appropriate.” Hansford agreed that the court need not adopt a test that allows inmates to challenge all changes to the level of custody and was content to have the court resolve Maxwell’s case by applying a narrower test endorsed by the government that would permit an inmate to use habeas to challenge the denial of an accelerated transfer to parole or, as here, a halfway house or home confinement.

Justice Samuel Alito pressed Hansford on whether an inmate may use Section 2241 simply to dispute how BOP calculated good-time credits or whether the inmate must also show that any error in calculating would also entitle the inmate to accelerated transfer to a halfway house or home confinement. After Hansford replied that Maxwell met all the requirements for the transfer and only the calculation of good-time credits was at issue, Justice Neal Gorsuch followed up by pressing whether the court needs to say that an inmate could bring a habeas challenge about the calculation of good-time credits alone. Hansford replied that the court did not need to say a dispute over good-time credits alone would suffice for a federal inmate to bring a claim in Section 2241.

Turning to Hansford’s level-of-custody test, Justice Sonia Sotomayor noted that, in a prior case, Justice Antonin Scalia had referred to habeas as covering a “‘quantum change’ in the level of custody,” but Hansford was asking the court “to create new language, ‘materially less restrictive custody.’ Why don’t we stick to what we have?” Hansford agreed that the court need not broaden its test and that Maxwell’s requested transfer to a halfway house or home confinement amounted to such a quantum change.

Assistant to the Solicitor General Kevin J. Barber, arguing for the government, contended that “there’s no need to adopt” Maxwell’s “amorphous level-of-custody rule.” He explained that the court “has always made clear that habeas is available when your claim is seeking discharge from actual physical confinement in prison to a different kind of custody,” such as a halfway house or home confinement. Here, the government relied heavily on Preiser v. Rodriguez, which held that habeas supplies the sole avenue for a state inmate to challenge the denial of credit toward release on parole.

When Gorsuch pressed Barber on the source of the government’s rule and why an inmate’s transfer to solitary confinement or maximum security would not be covered, Barber pointed principally to history and tradition in the court’s habeas cases. Barber explained, “we don’t have any tradition of claims being recognized as habeas claims challenging just transfers within prison systems.”

Despite agreeing that Maxwell could bring his claim in Section 2241, the government argued that no court could actually grant him relief. Specifically, the government pointed to 18 U.S.C. § 3621(b) and its terms barring courts from reviewing “a designation of a place of imprisonment under this subsection.” According to the government, service of a sentence in a halfway house or home confinement is “a designation of a place of imprisonment” governed by that statute.

But the government did not appear to have the votes to prevail on this argument, at least at the Supreme Court. Justice Brett Kavanaugh pressed Barber on whether that issue could be left for the lower court to decide on remand. Barber conceded that the court “absolutely could.” Sotomayor questioned Barber about whether the government is right that Section 3621(b) applies to Maxwell’s claim. But after Barber began responding, Justice Amy Coney Barrett reiterated Kavanaugh’s point that “we don’t have to get into any of this, right?” Barber again agreed.

Jackson pressed Barber on a separate point, that the BOP is expected to transfer Maxwell to a halfway house or home confinement in January 2027, which would soon make his claim moot. Jackson disputed why the court would revisit its decision to hear the case. Barber apologized for not pressing the Section 3621(b) issue or mootness in opposing certiorari, but said these factors spoke in favor of the court dismissing the case as improvidently granted.

The amicus, Brian W. Barnes, was appointed to argue in favor of the ruling by the U.S. Court of Appeals for the 5th Circuit that habeas is unavailable for Maxwell’s claim. When pressed by Justice Elena Kagan, Barnes agreed that habeas is available to “encompass a move from incarceration to parole,” as Preiser held. Kagan pointed out that “parole looks an awful lot like what’s going on here.”

The justices’ questions suggest that they are unlikely to limit Preiser and reject an analogy between Maxwell’s requested relief and the parole in that case. The justices also showed little appetite for reaching the government’s Section 3621(b) argument. How they would handle Maxwell’s expected release to a halfway house in January 2027 was less clear, but that circumstance could result in a speedier ruling.

Richard D. Cooke worked for the U.S. Department of Justice for 23 years, first at the Antitrust Division for 5 years and then as an Assistant United States Attorney in the Eastern District of Virginia for 18 years.

Essential Yoga Poses That Deliver Serious Knee Strengthening

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

The thing about practicing yoga for knee strengthening is you typically don’t know you need to do it until there’s an issue. YouTube yoga teacher Yoga With Kassandra takes you through a routine designed to emphasize the muscles that support knee strength and explains how to modify the poses to suit your needs.

Whether you’re wanting to build knee strength to enhance your everyday functionality or you’re recovery from a knee injury and finally at the point where you can practice yoga, you need strengthening exercises that support all the muscles that support and stabilize your knees. Basically, you want to work on the hip adductors, the quads, the glutes, the hamstrings.

The following poses literally work all of the muscles that contribute to knee stability. You’ll also get to play around a little with some dynamic movement and try some balancing exercises that help you understand how to properly engage the muscles that support the knee. Then you can take that awareness into other yoga poses as well as your everyday movements.

Regularly practicing this yoga for knee strength routine will help anyone build knee strength. I know from personal experience just how frustrating a knee injury can be, so if that applies to you, know that the more you dedicate time towards rehabilitating yourself, and the more diligent you are with your practices, the more likely you’ll feel a difference in your ease of movement. And remember that rest is equally as important as stretching and strengthening.

You will need a block or a block-like item for several of these poses. If you experience pain in any of the poses, slowly come out and skip ahead to the next pose. Always consult with your physician or physical therapist prior to introducing new exercises.

Start lying on your back and bring your feet about hip-distance apart and your heels pretty close toward your glutes. Place the block directly in between the inner thighs and squeeze the block as much as you possibly can. Then push down into all four corners of both feet and slowly lift first the pelvis off the mat, then the lower back, and then the mid back in Bridge Pose. The higher you lift, the harder it becomes to squeeze your block, but keep squeezing it anyway. This helps train your inner thighs, or adductor muscles, to contract. Building inner thigh power can also help you become better at balancing poses.

Take about 3 breaths here, pushing down through your big toes. Then exhale and slowly roll down, inch by inch. Move your block to the side.

From lying on your back, lift your right leg toward the sky and straighten it without locking your knee or hyperextending it. Interlace your fingers behind your right hamstrings. Instead of making this a passive stretch, push your leg against your palms as much as you can, and feel your arms resist. Keep pushing, pushing, pushing with your hamstrings. This should be challenging. You’ll also get a little arm workout. Try to keep your lower back neutral and close to the mat. Continue for a few more breaths.

You might find that your leg starts coming a little closer toward your chest, and that’s fine, but don’t force the stretch. Then release and switch legs.

Re-extend your right leg and bring your hands behind the back of your calf muscles. If that means you need to bend a little in your knee, that’s perfectly fine. If you can’t reach that far, bring your hands to the hamstrings again, but shift them closer to the knee, which creates a different sensation. Same thing, you want to push against your hands and resist. Then repeat on the other side.

This is not a yoga pose, but it’s absolutely amazing for your adductors. Grab your block and place it between your inner thighs again. Instead of keeping your heels close to you as in Bridge, walk your feet out so it feels almost as if your legs are almost straight and there’s about 6 inches between the mat and the backs of your knees.

Flex your feet, push your heels down into the mat, and see if you can squeeze your block and lift your hips off the mat. They’re only going to lift about an inch or so and that’s fine. This is an intense pose. You’re gonna feel it in your legs pretty much right away. If you don’t feel it, it’s because your knees are too bent and you need to straighten them a little more. Squeeze your block, push down into your heels, and try to drag your heels toward your shoulders. Hold here for a few more breaths. Then lower and move your block off to the side.

Come into Downward-Facing Dog. Normally I would suggest some Cat and Cow stretches before going into Down Dog, but if you’re recovering from knee issues you might not be able to apply bring your knees to the mat because of the pressure, so consider those stretches optional or take a seated version of them. You’re pushing into the heels here to stretch your hamstrings, lengthen your arms, and lengthen your spine.

Reach your right leg toward the sky in Three-Legged Dog, then step forward toward the top of the mat. Push down into your feet and come up into High Lunge. You want your feet hip-distance apart for stability. It’s find to bend your back knee if that feels more doable. It’s important that your front knee is going in the same direction as your toes and is stacked over your ankle. You don’t want the knee to be swaying to one side or jutting forward in front of your toes.

Bring your hands to your heart and keep your right knee bent and focus on its stability as you tilt your chest forward into a variation of Warrior 3. Keep the knee of your supporting leg bent as you reach your left leg back and find your balance.

As you inhale, push your right leg straight, and then exhale gently as you bend your right knee again. Think of this as almost like a one-legged squat. So inhale, straighten the leg. Exhale, bend the knee.

Continue for several breaths. Keep your right knee pointing straight over the second and third toes. Last one, inhale to straighten and hold here.

From Warrior 3, reach your left leg straight toward the ceiling and bring your chest toward your right thigh in Standing Splits. You might want to bring your block underneath your hands or fingertips.

Then exhale, bend both knees as much as you can, and see if you can bring your knees together to touch, so you’re in a little ball with your left foot hovering above the mat.

Then inhale, open back up into Standing Splits. Look at your right knee and make certain it’s not shifting to the right or the left. Do that once more.

Bend both knees and bring your left foot to the mat with your big toes together and your heels slightly apart in Chair Pose. If this creates tension in your lower back, you can keep your feet hip-distance apart. Rock your weight back into your heels and keep your tailbone reaching back and your thighs hugging the midline as if you were still squeezing that block. Stay here or reach your arms alongside your head.

Lean more weight onto your right leg, and see if you can pick up your left foot and step it back into your High Lunge. Then very carefully pivot the back foot almost parallel to the short side of the mat, keeping your shin and your knee facing the same direction, in Warrior 2. Your right knee is still bending. Squeezing the glutes helps press that right knee open as it points forward. Reach your arms straight out from your shoulders.

To work on strengthening, inhale as you straighten the front leg and bring your hands together in front of you, and then exhale back into Warrior 2 and reach your arms. Repeat several times. The goal is not to rush this but to do it with control. You want to really feel that knee tracking directly over the second and third toes and not wobbling.

Straighten your right leg, being careful not to lock or hyperextend your knees. Then shift forward into Triangle Pose and reach your left arm upward. Draw your left shoulder slightly back as you open across the chest.

From Triangle, look down at the floor and circle your hands to the mat. Step to Downward-Facing Dog.

Repeat these poses on your left side, starting with High Lunge and continuing to Dynamic Warrior 3, Standing Splits, Chair Pose, Dynamic Warrior 2, Triangle, and Down Dog.

From Down Dog, walk your feet to the top of the mat. Bend your knees a little, grab your block, and come all the way to standing. Come into Mountain Pose with the block in between your thighs, just as you did in Bridge Pose. Squeeze the block and hug in toward the midline. You can feel the adductors engage. Plant down firmly through all four corners of both feet.

As you inhale, stand a little taller. As you exhale, continue to squeeze your block as you lower yourself into Chair Pose. Repeat this movement about 5 times, moving slowly and steadily, continuing to squeeze the block. You might need to adjust your block a little.

Slowly fold forward in Standing Forward Bend. If you need to bend your knees, that’s perfectly fine. Even in this fold, can you squeeze your block? Then bend your knees a little more and release your block.

Slowly roll all the way up to standing. Notice how you feel as you attempt the pose, because if you’re still injured, the shape might not be accessible to you, and it’s fine to skip it. Lean onto your right foot, bend your left knee, and catch hold of the inner edge of your left foot behind you with your left hand. Keep your left hip and knee drawing inward toward the midline. So imagine you’re still squeezing your block in between your thighs. That is really important.

Reach your right arm forward. Instead of dipping the chest down and thinking of lifting the leg up, focus on pushing your foot into your palm. You’re not going super far forward in your Dancer Pose. You’re not working on flexibility, you’re working on strength, although you might feel an intense stretch along the front of your lifted quads.

To help with balance, you can always practice within arm’s reach of a chair or a wall so that you can reach out if needed for extra steadiness.

Then release and repeat on your other leg, foot into the right hand.

Make your way onto your belly on the mat. The shape of this pose is very similar to Dancer, so if this isn’t working for you, work on Locust Pose. If you want to do Bow Pose, bend your knees and see if you can catch hold of your feet and imagine you still have the block in between the inner upper thighs. Squeeze in and start to push the feet against your hands.

If you’re not doing Bow Pose, then bring your arms alongside your body, palms facing the mat, and slowly lift your feet and legs and chest off the mat Focus your gaze forward and down to keep the back of your neck long. Hold here or slowly release and then lift several times.

Flip over onto your back. Grab your block, lift your hips, slide your block underneath your pelvis, and just let yourself settle here. Slow down your breath and start to unwind.

When you’re ready, press into your feet, lift your pelvis, and move your block to the side before lowering yourself down. Maybe you draw your knees toward your belly and bring your arms underneath your calves and stay still or rock a little side to side.

Extend your arms and legs, close your eyes, and notice how you feel now as opposed to when you first started this practice. Let yourself settle into this space.

When you’re ready, begin to lengthen your breath and wiggle your fingers and toes. You can reach your arms up overhead, take a big stretch, and roll onto one side. Come up to take a seat, sitting up tall, maybe closing your eyes for a moment.

Thank you so much for doing this knee-strengthening sequence with me. I hope that it gave you some ideas as to how you can strengthen your knees.

According to CoinGlass data, approximately $1 billion in leveraged positions had been liquidated over the previous 24 hours, affecting more than 201,000 traders.

The Kobeissi Letter estimated that approximately $2.5 trillion had been wiped from US stock market valuations and reported that the Cboe Volatility Index, Wall Street's closely watched fear gauge, had surged more than 70%.

The market commentary firm attributed the speed of the collapse to excessive leverage meeting an abrupt change in investor sentiment, rather than a sudden deterioration in corporate fundamentals.

The selloff followed the Nov. 4 rally, when investors initially welcomed the Democrats' sweeping midterm victories as a potential constraint on President Donald Trump's economic policies.

However, attention quickly shifted toward the implications for the artificial intelligence industry, whose enormous capital expenditure commitments have helped sustain US equity valuations.

Investors are increasingly concerned that tighter political oversight, rising construction costs, and local opposition to data centers could undermine the expected growth in AI infrastructure spending.

“Such a ban, if even possible to enforce, would effectively mean the US surrendering its position in the global AI race to China.”

Those concerns have gained traction following Tuesday's elections. According to Ballotpedia, 46 of the 47 local data-center-related measures decided across the US in 2026 favored tighter restrictions, although some votes were advisory rather than legally binding.

The political backlash threatens to complicate an investment cycle built on expectations of continued demand for AI chips, electricity, computing infrastructure and data-center construction.

Nvidia fell below $200 for the first time since August, while the Nasdaq 100 declined approximately 5.5%, putting it on course for its steepest daily loss since April 2025.

Economist Mohamed El-Erian questioned whether the rest of the US economy could maintain its momentum if technology investment slowed.

He also cautioned against assuming that Democratic control of Congress would necessarily translate into a substantial slowdown in the technology sector.

Meanwhile, Citrini Research argued that expectations for AI infrastructure spending may have peaked as investors reassess whether projected returns can justify the industry's enormous capital requirements.

The research firm clarified that it was identifying a potential peak in spending estimates.

The distinction is significant for Bitcoin, which has increasingly traded alongside technology stocks during periods of financial market stress.

Thursday's simultaneous collapse in technology shares and cryptocurrency prices suggests investors are reducing exposure to speculative assets as uncertainty over AI investment returns, financing costs, and future regulation intensifies.

The next test comes as markets assess whether the political backlash will materially slow data-center construction or merely reduce expectations for how quickly the AI industry can expand.

UK weighs ban on Meta Ray-Bans and other smart glasses, citing 'secret filming'

The United Kingdom is joining a growing list of countries looking to potentially restrict or outright ban smart glasses, especially in public spaces. Satvir Kaur, the country's Safeguarding minister, said in a Guardian report, "The government is looking into options to restrict smart glasses in certain settings and restricting the sale to certain users."

Kaur added that the benefits of smart glasses have to be weighed against their harms. In England and Wales, Meta glasses were recently banned in courtrooms, much like a similar ban in New York state. The courts promised to confiscate the glasses if they were worn into the courtroom (and returned after upon leaving).

According to Kaur, England's National Health Service and Department for Education are separately looking into policies regarding smart glasses.

In the last few months, smart glasses, like the Ray-Ban Meta, have faced a public backlash, even earning the moniker "pervert glasses."

In a debate about the glasses, MP Anne Sabina said, "Women are reporting waking up after nights out to find videos of themselves have gone viral online, filmed without their knowledge or consent by men wearing smart glasses."

Whether or not a ban is enacted, Britain is adding to a growing chorus of governments and agencies looking at restricting smart glasses.

Australia's government announced in September that it was barring the use of camera-equipped glasses in government workspaces. The EU is scrutinizing glasses, though nothing official has been pursued yet.

Get instant access to breaking news, the hottest reviews, great deals and helpful tips.

Norway is reportedly planning a temporary ban on smart glasses in public spaces

In the United States, even ICE has banned Ray-Ban Meta glasses for its agents, citing privacy and security concerns. With the recent "blue wave" in the 2026 midterm elections, Silicon Valley and AI are expected to face increased scrutiny. More than likely, smart glasses will be included in that backlash.

Even Kaur pointed out that built-in cameras and displays could be beneficial for visually impaired individuals. "Smart glasses have been a game-changer, helping them navigate the world around them," Kaur said.

However, smart glasses don't necessarily need cameras to be beneficial.

Meta claims it was working on its camera-free Ray-Ban Meta Audio for years before their debut at Meta Connect 2026. Perhaps the company was, but at the time it seemed to launch in time to combat some of the pervert glasses criticisms.

Several Tom's Guide writers have tried the Audio-only Meta glasses, and we've all come away with the same praise: it was the right call.

"Taking out the camera has made me an even bigger fan of Ray Ban Meta glasses," AI editor Amanda Caswell said after 72 hours with the glasses.

There is a way for smart glasses to gain traction, and perhaps ditching the camera could help.

Christian Horner Doesn’t Sound Like He’s Done With Formula 1 Yet

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

“I feel like I’m too young to retire,” he says.

“How would you introduce yourself to somebody that doesn’t know you?” I ask the man across the table.

“Christian Horner,” he says, then without pause, “somebody that used to be involved in Formula 1.”

Considering this is a man who could have truthfully responded “Commander of the Order of the British Empire” or “husband of Ginger Spice,” the choice to define himself by his past rather than present or future is hard to miss. His is a familiar name and a familiar face to even casual F1 fans. The Brit’s sharp tongue and vaguely Daniel Craig-ish visage made him a lead character of Netflix’s Drive to Survive in its first years when his Red Bull Formula 1 team was banking win after win, thanks also to the talents of people like Max Verstappen and Adrian Newey.

But no hot streak lasts forever, no matter how good you (or the people you work with) are. Horner was ejected from the team after the 2025 British Grand Prix in the wake of both a sexual harassment accusation scandal the year before and a general downturn in the team’s performance, reportedly receiving a €60 million platinum parachute in exchange for his departure from the sport.

For most, that might seem a sign from the universe to take up a hobby—say, gentleman farmer. But Horner isn’t most men. He’s still out there, orbiting F1 like a geosynchronous satellite: a long ways off but still locked above it, looking down at the whole of the world he left. As such, he’s been speaking at industry events like Sportico Invest London and the Autosport Business Exchange in New York, where I sat down with him before he went on stage.

Unsurprisingly to anyone who saw him on Drive to Survive, or at any of the 10,000 media events he did during his F1 career, Horner still has plenty to say. Even if he is trying to be polite about it.

“The racing has been entertaining, don’t get me wrong. I’m watching these races and seeing cars overtaking in Melbourne and other circuits that you don’t normally overtake at,” he says when discussing the current F1 season. “But they’re not really overtakes as opposed to ... drive-pasts.”

He is not, it seems, a particular fan of the 2026 power unit regulations that have caused consternation amongst teams and fans alike by forcing the gas-electric hybrid propulsion systems to split their output 50/50 between internal combustion-sourced and electron-derived power. Horner describes it as the biggest issue for F1 to tackle at the moment. “You would assume it would be a relatively easy fix, just to change that ratio, but it seems a little more complicated,” he says. “You hear the drivers not liking how these cars are to drive, because you’re not rewarded for driving quickly through a fast corner, for example.”

“I think they’re doing the right thing for 2030,” he adds, referring to FIA president Mohammed Ben ‌Sulayem’s plans to ditch the current hybrid V-6 powertrains for V-8 units. “I think it’s unfortunate for the current presidency because he inherited this set of regulations. So of course, he’s looking to address that for the next round of significant change to engine spec.”

The 2026 season’s power unit frustrations haven’t been all bad, at least in terms of entertainment value. They’ve played a role in keeping the constructors’ championship battle interesting, even if the driver’s championship was swept away by wunderkind Kimi Antonelli.

“It’s interesting, I think different teams have had different moments this year,” he says. “Mercedes started very strong; Ferrari then had a moment where they looked like they were gonna put a challenge together around Barcelona time—seeing Lewis [Hamilton] win his first race, Charles [Leclerc] winning at Silverstone. And McLaren started to get their act together, looked pretty racy around Zanvoort and Budapest time, and then obviously, the Red Bull cars have come on strong.”

But those cars at front of the field are practically in a different league from the other seven teams in this season, which Horner—despite his own successes captaining Red Bull teams that ran the table with Brady-Belichick efficiency—says isn’t ideal for the sport. “I think you want as many cars in the fight as you can get, and the best way to achieve that is stability. Because then they all [close on] the same solutions, and just iterate those same solutions. So if you want close racing, just leave those regulations, those chassis regulations, alone. And you’ll see them all converge onto one solution—the better solution.”

In other words, simplify, then add budget. Formula 1, as he points out, is both one of the most expensive sports in the world and the biggest team sport on the planet. “You’ve got over 2000 team members between engine and chassis, all producing two cars for twenty-four races a year.” Running all as team principal, unsurprisingly, is like spinning plates and juggling bowling pins at the same time, even before factoring in the spotlight.

“Challenges ... there are many. There’s the scrutiny you come under. The politics, the competition of people trying to nick your staff, your drivers, your sponsors. It’s such a competitive industry. There’s so much at stake with large amounts of money involved” he says. “It’s dog-eat-dog in that respect. You gotta get up pretty early in the morning to stay ahead of the rest.”

Having done so for so many years—and most of those seeing an above-average amount of success for the position—certainly suggests the 52-year-old Brit has what Liam Neeson might describe as a particular set of skills. Would you, I ask him, eventually like to circle back to Formula 1?

“I think ...” Here, he pauses. “You know, I had an incredible run. I’m very proud of what I did. It didn’t end the way in the way I would have liked. I’m still relatively young ... I would only go back—knowing the sacrifices it takes—for the right project. Only if I was in a position where I could influence change, drive change within the organization, with like-minded people that wanted to win ... I’ve won close to one-third of the races I entered. I don’t want to go back just to take part.”

But he’s not pigeonholing himself. “I have time on my side to be able to make decisions. I feel like I’m too young to retire,” he says. “Honestly, sitting right here, right now, I have no idea where the next twelve months are going to take me. And I find that kind of exciting.”

Will Sabel Courtney is a contributing editor at Road & Track. A 12-year veteran of journalism, he formerly worked at Gear Patrol, The Drive, and RIDES Magazine, and contributed to the New York Daily News and Jalopnik, among other outlets. You can often find him test-driving new cars in New York City, cursing the slow-moving traffic surrounding him.

BioWare Documentary Slated for Release in 2027

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

Daniel is a lifelong gamer with a love for various genres and gameplay styles who enjoys exploring games and playing from various angles.

It’s been a few weeks now since the sudden announcement of EA’s closure of BioWare, longtime developer of the fan-favorite Mass Effect series. The major fervor surrounding this landmark closure, as well as the cancellation of the next Mass Effect, has gradually started to die down, and all that’s left is a sad little hole in our hearts where hope for a new spacefaring adventure once lived.

In spite of its unfortunate denouement, it doesn’t change the fact that BioWare was, is, and shall always remain a beloved studio, whose stories and worlds touched many a heart. In fact, BioWare and its staff were something of hometown heroes to its origin in the city of Edmonton, Canada. Because of this, a production effort has been spurred forth by the Canadian entertainment industry to properly document the studio’s 30-plus years of history.

For an in-depth look at how we lost one of the gaming greats, this is how the fall of BioWare was years in the making.

According to a Deadline exclusive published today, a documentary outlining the history of BioWare, from its founding in 1995 to its closure this year, is in production now, with hopes to have it up on screens sometime next year, including a run through all of the major film festivals. This will be the definitive BioWare story, told not just by its staff, but the many fans whose lives were changed by its productions.

The documentary, titled “Massive Effect: The BioWare Story,” is being written by Canadian writer and director Akash Sherman, best known for his 2018 sci-fi film, Clara. Interestingly, the actual filming process for this documentary began about two years ago, which means everything was already well underway the moment the decision came down from EA to shutter the studio. Sherman and his crew have captured the moment the BioWare story came to an end, as well as the rippling effects it had on the gaming community.

The actual filming process for this documentary began about two years ago.

The headliners of the documentary are BioWare’s three original founders, Ray Muzyka, Greg Zeschuk, and Augustine Yip, though it’s also slated to feature appearances by a variety of other game industry alumni, including Lucasfilm Games Vice President Douglas Reilly, Star Wars: Knights of the Old Republic director Casey Hudson, Game Awards creator Geoff Keighley, and even EA’s infamous former CEO, John Riccitiello.

A ranking list of all the games BioWare has ever developed in the history of the studio.

“When the production team asked for our blessing to tell the BioWare story, we were honored and deeply moved,” Muzyka and Zeschuk said in a statement obtained by Deadline. “BioWare was never just about the logo or the building. It was about the stories brought to life by the amazing people on our dev teams, and the fans who defended those worlds, championed them and carried them forward.”

The film’s creation and production is something of a love letter from BioWare’s home turf of Canada. In fact, its creation was commissioned by Canada’s Super Channel, with the Canada Media Fund offering support throughout the production.

“We are thrilled to bring Massive Effect: The BioWare Story to Canadian audiences,” Kim Ball, EVP of Content & Communications for Super Channel told Deadline. “As an Edmonton-based company, we’re especially proud to celebrate BioWare’s extraordinary hometown story and the people, creativity and groundbreaking storytelling behind some of the most beloved games of a generation.”

The film’s producers are inviting BioWare’s fans to contribute photos, videos, and stories of how BioWare’s games have affected their lives, with a select few slated to be featured in the film. “BioWare’s fans have been part of its journey since the very first game,” said one of the film’s producers, Adam Scorgie. “It’s only right that they help us tell the rest of the story.”

Sherman himself has similar reverence for BioWare, saying that their games are what helped ignite his initial creative spark, and that it was his “privilege and responsibility to share the journey of underdogs who came together to create deeply beloved storytelling.”

There's still a chance that Mass Effect can survive.

French authorities investigate claim of fraud over Picasso plates and medallions on sale

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

Pablo Picasso with a silver plate, in 1957. The artist had commissioned François Hugo to produce repoussé metal pieces from his designs © 2026 Succession Picasso/DACS, London; © Photo: Edward Quinn, © edwardquinn.com

A criminal investigation amid a family crisis casts a dark shadow over the illustrious history of the French studio Ateliers Hugo, which produced jewellery and silverware created by major artists of the 20th century. According to a legal source, the court of Aix-en-Provence in the south of France has opened an investigation following a complaint by the Picasso family.

On 10 March, Paloma Ruiz-Picasso, representing the nine heirs of the Spanish artist, filed a complaint alleging “fraud, counterfeiting, forgery and concealment of these felonies”. The file does not name the alleged perpetrators, and their identity is not presently known. The following month the Picasso family published a formal warning in the Gazette Drouot, titled “Hugo silverware”. It urged “all art market professionals and buyers to exercise the utmost vigilance”, following the appearance of what it described as “a large number” of illegitimate silver plates and gold medallions, valued at millions of euros.

Paloma Ruiz-Picasso, the daughter of Pablo, has filed a legal complaint on behalf of nine family members of the artist about the allegedly unauthorised works © Studio Cabrelli

The title refers to Ateliers Hugo, a goldsmith based in Aix-en-Provence. It was founded in 1933 by François Hugo (1899-1981), the great-grandson of the famous French author Victor Hugo. In 1955 Pablo Picasso, who since 1947 had created a variety of ceramics in Vallauris on the French Riviera, commissioned François Hugo to produce plates and dishes from similar designs in fine metals, using the traditional technique of repoussé. Later, the workshop reduced them into medallions and earrings. With the help of his brother, the painter Jean Hugo, who introduced him to artistic circles, François made jewellery designed by Salvador Dalí, Max Ernst, André Derain, Jean Arp, Jean Dubuffet, Roberto Matta, Jean Lurçat and Dorothea Tanning. In 1975, his son Pierre Hugo (1947-2023) took over the company. Pierre was followed by his son Nicolas (born 1989).

Members of the Picasso family and their lawyer, Jean-Jacques Neuer, who filed the complaint, declined to comment to The Art Newspaper. But their complaint alleges that “it has been discovered that the moulds of Picasso’s works were reused for unauthorised productions of silver and gold pieces, followed by duplicates, some of them bearing three times the same number, and unnumbered specimens”.

According to a source close to the investigation, the Picasso Administration, which represents the family, says it has discovered at least 17 complete or almost complete suspicious collections, amounting to hundreds of pieces. The first red flag was raised by the Museum of Old and New Art (Mona), a private Australian museum in Tasmania. According to this source, in May 2023 the museum informed the Picasso Administration of its doubts over plates it had bought at Sotheby’s in London. An auction catalogue of 13 October 2021 describes a “complete set of 24 silver plates” bearing the signature of the artist, completed with an additional “unnumbered proof of Visage aux mains”. The pieces were “accompanied by a certificate of authenticity issued by Pierre Hugo”. The lot was sold for £1.4m. Sotheby’s declined to comment for this story.

According to the legal files, the seller was the British dealer Mark Maurice, from a now dissolved gallery named Masterpiece. The source close to the investigation says the dealer allegedly produced documents showing that he had commissioned a set of plates and a set of medallions from the Ateliers Hugo between 2017 and 2020. Mark Maurice indicated he was “unable to respond” to The Art Newspaper’s inquiries “due to strict non-disclosure agreements in place”.

In summer 2023 the ceramics museum in Vallauris, Musée Magnelli, exhibited another collection of 24 silver plates as well as gold medallions attributed to Picasso.

In 2023, the Musée Magnelli in Vallauris exhibited plates and medallions attributed to Picasso Travelworld

According to the legal files, the Picasso Administration protested, having found that they bore no number, mark or punch, as legally required. Nicolas Hugo acknowledges these facts, but tells The Art Newspaper that the works exhibited at Musée Magnelli were so-called “author’s copies, made for the exhibition, duly indicating in the catalogue that they were not for sale”. He adds that he “recovered them and had them melted after the show”. Representatives for the museum did not respond to a request for comment.

In June 2023, as reported in the legal files, the French branch of the auction house Bonhams made a request to the Picasso Administration for the right to reproduce silverware for a catalogue. The auction house was planning a sale of 95 lots from the Ateliers Hugo collection, including works by Ernst, Derain and Cocteau, valued between €400 and €50,000 each. The most valuable were 59 pieces, “made by” or “after” Picasso.

On 26 September 2023 Bonhams was informed by the Picasso Administration that these pieces were considered to be “unauthorised” editions. The auction house then decided to “delay the whole sale”, just two days before it was due to take place. Nicolas Hugo acknowledges he “did initiate, in the wake of good results obtained at Bonhams UK, this sale of pieces”, produced, he says, by his “father and grandfather”. But, he adds, “When we were informed by dealers of the presence of possible duplicates, I agreed to withdraw the sale and collaborated with the Picasso Administration to identify them.”

Bonhams declined to comment, but according to the source close to the investigation, the lots attributed to Picasso were sequestered when a British financial company, Suros Capital, tried to recover them as collateral for a €256,000 loan. A spokesperson for Suros confirmed the operation to The Art Newspaper, adding that “it was secured against not only the Picasso pieces, but a range of assets which are progressively realised, so the company expects to recover the loan in full”.

In November 2023, according to the source close to the investigation, a representative for Bonhams France informed the Picasso Administration that a Spanish collector contacted the auction house to sell 24 silver plates, estimated at £900,000. Again, the auction house refused on the basis of Picasso Administration’s doubts about the objects’ authenticity. In the legal files, the Picasso Administration claims the same collector had sold another set of 24 silver plates and one of 24 gold medallions in private sales through Bonhams in London in 2022. Neither the British auction house nor the Spanish collector made any comment.

Nicolas Hugo, who says he has not been interviewed by the investigators, claims he has no knowledge of the proceedings and “is unaware of the content of the complaint” issued by the Picasso Administration. But, he says: “The criminal investigation can only deal with facts related to the time of my father and grandfather, therefore falling under the statute of limitations and concerning persons now deceased.” He adds that “the rights to reproduce the items disappeared with them, so I have never minted any of Picasso’s creations”, and he does not have the legal status of a goldsmith. However, he says: “I have signed some certificates of authenticity, but only to confirm my father’s own signature.”

In interviews with The Art Newspaper, Nicolas Hugo describes himself as a “self-taught art dealer”. When he was around 20 years old, he started to work as an assistant in galleries in Paris, before moving to London. He is now an agent for several artists and a curator. In 2022, the French magazine Les Hardis described him as a “mix of British and Saint-Germain-des-Prés charm”. “My father became very ill in 2018,” he told the magazine. “I had an epiphany. I decided to take over the workshop. I immersed myself completely,” and started to train in manufacturing jewels.

“From 2019,” he tells The Art Newspaper, “I became involved in the history of his workshop and helped my father to showcase this heritage, for memorial and commercial reasons. I set up exhibitions in places like Barcelona or Vallauris and initiated this sale of the fonds d’atelier at Bonhams Paris. I have not taken over my father’s company and was not his partner.”

Nicolas Hugo says: “There have been some duplicates on the market, dating from my father’s period, but I have no idea who had made them in the workshop.” He says he has “no idea who put a collection on sale at Sotheby’s in 2021” and knows “nothing of the three sets proposed by this Spanish collector to Bonhams in 2022 and 2023”.

The criminal investigation is still at the preliminary phase. It comes at a time when the company is already in limbo. Pierre Hugo died in October 2023 and his five children have turned down his inheritance. Nicolas Hugo’s brothers and sisters declined to comment to The Art Newspaper due to the ongoing investigation.

However, sources close to the case say that Pierre Hugo’s heirs’ inheritance came with a €2.5m tax debt. According to these sources, three of the Hugo siblings said in police interviews they were “not at all” aware of the conduct of the workshop by their brother Nicolas, who had succeeded their ailing father in 2019. The sources said the three Hugo siblings were on alert after learning that Bonhams Paris declined to sell the collection from the Ateliers Hugo in September 2023. According to the same sources, when Pierre Hugo died a month later, the three siblings went to the workshop and took 21 moulds of plates that they decided to hand over to the Picasso Administration. Three moulds from the set were missing and they did not find those for the medallions.

According to these sources, the three Hugo siblings explained to the investigators that, “knowing that the rights of reproduction were extinct, they wanted to be sure this would be respected”. The siblings also asserted that they “did not want in any way to be implicated in these operations and made it clear to the Picasso Administration”, according to the sources.

Together with his wife Monique Mueller, the Swiss collector built one of the largest private holdings of Pre-Columbian, African and Oceanic objects in Europe

Japanese enjoy art more than French, says curator of Lille Métropole Museum of Modern Art

The third-generation Ateliers Hugo has been casting artists’ creations using ancient techniques from the same workshop in the south of France for nearly 70 years

Gray Zone Warfare Update Addresses Biggest Rogue Ops Concerns

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

Lauren has experience in the gaming and esports space, writing news, guides, features, reviews, lists, conducting interviews, and more. Before joining the team at DualShockers, Lauren was a Features Writer at Adventure Gamers, a Guides Writer at Gameranx, as well as contributing as a News Writer at PCInvasion and Daily

Gray Zone Warfare stands out for its immersion as a PvE open-world tactical shooter set on a quarantined Southeast Asian island with plenty of narrative elements to get stuck into. Rogue Ops allows for a fresh experience by building your very own Hideout and growing your reputation in new ways.

The ambitious mode has a lot to love, but it has faced its fair share of player complaints, as major issues have been interrupting the fun in the gameplay loop. A Halloween Dev Stream teased significant improvements to enemy AI and making the Hideout economy more rewarding.

This is the detailed all purpose hub for Gray Zone Warfare guides and content.

Now that Gray Zone Warfare Patch 0.5.1.0 is live, we can dive into all the key changes you can expect to see when you embark on your next mission.

First of all, huge changes have been made to the way enemy AI behaves. One of the biggest criticisms players had of the AI enemies was their inconsistency and the feeling of them being extremely overpowered.

Following this update, enemies should no longer know your exact position after shooting. Sound now only gives them an approximate location, meaning the farther away you are, the more difficult it is for them to detect you. Rather than the AI instantly sharing your exact position, they'll rely on information they can gather, which ultimately makes their reactions more natural.

Stealth has also undergone some work to make it more consistent. Moving slowly should make you harder to detect, and the AI exhibits reactions to light that you’d expect. These changes should make stealth, flanking enemies, and repositioning more rewarding and allow you to take a greater advantage over your surroundings.

Improvements have also been made to how enemies engage in combat. Trained soldiers bring more precision to their combat abilities and make better use of cover, but untrained enemies will be more careless in their actions.

Combined with fixing pesky problems with hit boxes, grenades, and enemies getting stuck behind doors, this series of changes aims to make combat more varied and test your tactical performance.

Sound now only gives them an approximate location, meaning the farther away you are, the more difficult it is for them to detect you.

Another major change has been made to the economy in Hideout. A range of rewards have been increased, including raising the rewards from smuggling tasks and increasing the sales prices of smuggling backpacks, and improving rewards from hideout handovers and increasing the sale prices of hideout products.

Production and disassembly times have even been reduced across the board, and the size and weight of selected building materials and disassembly have been reduced for a better looting experience.

The Gray Zone Warfare update arrives with a slew of bug fixes to gameplay, animations, audio, tasks, and so much more. This includes fixing a bug where the health panel could show 100% damage on an organ that was not destroyed and repairing an issue that caused some tasks to not be offered due to a nearby task blocking them. The full patch notes can be viewed here.

In the meantime, the team will continue to address further feedback, like AI enemies seemingly taking too many shots to kill, provided as an example, with further fixes planned for upcoming patches. The developers are already working on their next major update, Patch 0.5.2.0.

Wyndham Is Giving Members Free Hotel Status Upgrades—Some Can Claim 3,000 Points Instead

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

You have to register, and the gift depends on your current status, where you live, and whether you’re already enrolled in Wyndham’s Insider subscription.

I don’t get excited about Wyndham status, but free status and free points are good things. If you don’t have hotel status today, having something could be an avenue to match somewhere else. So it could be worth a couple of clicks, even more so if you have a Wyndham stay coming up.

Claim the status upgrade by December 30, 2026. It lasts through December 31, 2026, and Wyndham says to allow 24–48 hours for it to take effect. (Do it on December 30 and it may activate and expire simultaneously – yay!) New members can join and claim Gold. Note that you can’t use the promotional status to match to Caesars Rewards.

The upgraded earning rate is determined by your status at checkout. A stay ending January 1 won’t earn the holiday status bonus.

Wyndham normally requires 5 qualifying nights for Gold, 15 for Platinum, and 40 for Diamond. The benefits are modest:

Preferred room choice means a better location or view within the room category you booked. Early check-in and late checkout are subject to availability, generally up to two hours before or after the hotel’s stated time. Diamond suite upgrades depend on availability at check-in.

As I explained when I looked at Wyndham’s instructions to hotels, you should ask for the preferred room and checkout benefits. And there’s no program-wide elite breakfast or lounge benefit. At a hotel earning the usual 10 base points per dollar, moving from Platinum to Diamond adds just 50 points on $100 of eligible spending. That’s 35 cents at a valuation of 0.7 cents per point.

Eligible Diamonds need to claim their 3,000 points by December 31, 2026.

Members who become Diamond through this holiday upgrade don’t qualify for the points gift. Neither do members with a competitor status match unless they’ve completed the required 10 qualifying nights within 90 days of receiving that matched status.

Wyndham’s messaging on this says 3,000 points are enough for a discounted night at any participating hotel. That doesn’t actually match their current award chart.

Following the September 15 devaluation, standard free nights cost 5,000, 15,000, 30,000 or 45,000 points per bedroom. The points plus cash price at a 45,000-point hotel starts at 4,500 points plus the cash portion.

Ultimately Wyndham has lost much of what made it interesting to me: Vacasa redemptions are gone, the easy credit card route to Caesars Diamond ended, and the top award price rose 50%.

Wyndham Rewards Insider normally costs $95 per year and automatically renews. It offers hotel discounts, extra points, and access to deals on flights, cruises, rental cars, activities and entertainment, along with a concierge.

The advertised 50% increase in earning applies to the elite bonus. A Diamond’s bonus rises from 20% to 30%, so ordinary earning at a 10-point-per-dollar hotel goes from 12 to 13 points per dollar or 8.3% more points. And the hotel discount isn’t a promise of the lowest price. Wyndham acknowledges that another rate available to non-Insiders can be cheaper. Buying Platinum could make sense for someone who isn’t, can’t status match, but still plans a bunch of Wyndham stays upcoming.

The holiday upgrade can be combined with Wyndham’s current 4X points promotion. Register before booking, book direct by January 15, 2027, and complete eligible paid stays by January 19. The holiday status itself ends December 31. The multiplier applies to base points, with a cap of 15,000 promotional bonus points. At the normal 10-point base rate, a non-Insider Diamond earns 40 points per dollar from the base earning and promotion, plus 2 elite bonus points (not four times their usual 12). Insiders can earn another 5,000 points after two qualifying nights, either together or across two stays. That bonus doesn’t stack with the separate Wyndham cardholder bonus. Premier cardholders can earn 10,000 instead. For an upcoming stay, claim your status first and register for the points promotion before making the reservation since a booking made before promotion registration doesn’t qualify. Borg Decks Dominated Our Star Trek Early Access MTG Drafts

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

We've just finished five drafts in the Magic: The Gathering Star Trek early access window on MTG Arena, and we have some initial thoughts on how the set drafts via the Premier Draft and Early Access Sealed game modes.

In short, it's a poor experience compared to Reality Fracture, but that also feels like a very high bar to measure against, and if we were instead to look only at other Universes Beyond drafts we've done this year, it's probably just behind The Hobbit and TNMT, while being better than Marvel Super Heroes.

Well, we can't say this was entirely unexpected, but drafting Borg Artifact decks in Star Trek will be quite the challenge in pick-one and pick-two situations. Throughout our early-access Star Trek drafts, it became clear everyone was fighting over the Borg and artifact cards, with our only real free picks coming in the first two or three slots.

This despite picking up Locutus, the One Who Speaks in pack one, pick one, the rest of our choices were too scattered and sporadic, leading to a mismatched deck that really struggled to stay on theme. Still, we drafted enough Borg and artifact creature cards to assemble a competent deck; we just ran into three straight Borg mirror matches after an early run of wins against red-green Landfall and green-white doctor decks, and that ended the first draft on a sour note.

We have ten draft archetypes in total, one for each traditional MTG guild or dual-color combo, with some looking far more exciting than others.

We felt infinitely more optimistic about this deck, given that it didn't feel like we were competing with anyone else in the draft class for the cards that worked best in this archetype, but after the first few picks, all dilemma cards appeared to dry up, or came in mana colors we simply didn't want to stretch to.

The downfall of our first deck came because we felt forced to go with three colors rather than two, and the same ultimately happened here, and we had to force a few red dilemma cards into the deck, then hope that we had done enough to still try and grab a few wins against the inevitable Borg wave coming my way.

Luckily, we had done enough to actually pick up a few wins with this deck, and while it's awfully reliant on you drawing your dilemma-reactive cards like The Caretaker, Melkotian Adjudicator, Obsidian Order Operative, and Elim Garak, Simple Tailor early, we packed in enough card draw to ensure that we always had a hand full of cards and were able to also play creatures or spells to stave off any early aggression.

That said, because of the nature of draft and having only a 40-card deck, by the time we had our big threats in hand, we often had little protection, so we had to hope our opponent had no answers to our creatures and spells.

Frustrated with how the Premier Drafts were playing out, we switched to sealed events, which, instead of drafting as a group, give you six Play Boosters to use to assemble your deck. We returned to the Borg archetype, having come up against it in more than half our previous matches across two drafts, and it became clear why it's proving so popular.

You can draft cards to build a really effective mana curve, so you can play from turn one and increase your power on the battlefield every turn. As much as we love the idea of strong archetypes standing out above the crowd, when one in particular is being so heavily played, it can suck the fun out of the draft modes in general as it all becomes too repetitive.

While not officially an archetype, the second round of sealed play gave us the cards we needed to build an aggressive red-white deck that used cheap but effective instant and sorcery spells and creatures that could snowball quickly. We still had some of the power 8+ cards, but not enough to say the deck was built around them.

The key, however, was finding a Flare of Fortitude in the packs, which let us protect all cards on the battlefield and our life total, and we didn't even have to pay the mana cost as long as we could sacrifice a white creature. This card was key to every victory, as it often caught opponents out when we were fully tapped and passing the turn.

Ultimately, the red-white build surprised us with how effective it can be, and while it's not as aggressive as some of the red-black Klingon decks we faced, it still managed to reach six wins under our control.

We returned to Premier Draft for the final run of the session, and it was a lot more fruitful than the previous two. Going into this draft knowing we wanted to avoid Borgs at all costs led to much more success in drafting an archetypewe were happy with.

Having played against a few Klingon decks before drafting one, it was a little easier to scope out the must-haves, and we also knew being ultra-aggressive was the best way forward. We wanted our opponents on the ropes or potentially beaten by turn four or five, and that's how most of the wins played out. The losses, as you might expect, came from games that went much longer; we simply ran out of steam, and our opponents eventually found answers to our bigger threats and controlled the game from then on.

Klingons were ultimately the archetype we had the most fun with, and that goes a long way toward making the draft experience feel much better overall.

We're basing this off the decks that we built and the decks that we played against. It's not a fully committed ranking, but just our snapshot impressions after 40 games played across these five drafts (and a few more unrecorded since).

Sheffield United, despite a difficult summer transfer window, have bounced back with aplomb and cannot be ruled out of a play-off spot this season, and one player will be crucial to those efforts.

Sheffield United underwent a difficult summer window, with each of Andre Brooks, Gustavo Hamer, and Callum O'Hare leaving the club to bring in some much-needed funds, as loans and free transfers were heavily relied upon as a means to bring in recruits.

Ben Brereton Diaz returned to South Yorkshire on loan from Southampton, whilst Matt Doherty, Jordan Ayew, Romelle Donovan, Kalvin Phillips, Hamza Choudhury, and Liam Kitching followed.

Whilst the majority of those have had a solid enough impact at this stage, there is one player who has caught the eye more than most and could command a lucrative sum.

Through AI, we'll rank the top 25 most intimidating football stadiums in British football, but which stadium comes out on top?

Sydie Peck has been in the headlines as of late for his surprising first-half hat-trick against Preston North End in midweek, but the midfielder's displays throughout this campaign deserve more recognition.

Coming through the Arsenal academy before joining the Blades in 2021, the 22-year-old has made over a century of appearances for the South Yorkshire outfit across all competitions since, and has gone on to become one of the first names on the team sheet under Chris Wilder in recent years.

A regular in the England youth setup, Peck is bound for the Premier League at some stage of his career, and with four goals and two assists in 14 Championship games from midfield already this season, it's no surprise January interest in the midfielder is expected.

Football League World's Sheffield United fan pundit, Jimmy from Blades Ramble, has shared their thoughts on whether Peck will attract interest from elsewhere in the near future, and if so, how much the Blades should demand for the midfielder.

Jimmy said: "I fully expect interest in Sydie Peck in January. He's a good player, and I think Chris Wilder noted in his post-match interview that he needed to add goals to his game, and he started to do that.

"So a hat-trick, very impressive, but really his overall game has improved, and I definitely think he has the ability to become a Premier League player.

"I would prefer it if he stayed under the radar a little bit and stayed with Sheffield United. What I do think, though, is, now that there's no longer a need to sell at Bramall Lane, if Sydie Peck does get an offer that we find too good to refuse, then I think Wilder will be given a lot of the funds to strengthen the squad.

"We've been in this position before when we sold David Brooks, and we brought in players like John Egan and Ollie Norwood. But what is that price? I think it's at least £15 million. When you look at some of the prices that players are going for at this level now, he's an England U21 international who just scored a hat-trick in the Championship; at least £15 million. I'd be holding out for £20 million."

Do you agree with Jimmy's take on Sydie Peck? How much do you think Sheffield United should be commanding for the midfielder if interest were to arrive as early as January? Share your thoughts below!

Having been forced to part ways with Brooks, Hamer, and O'Hare in the summer, Sheffield United and their head coach Chris Wilder will have little intention of losing any other stars anytime soon.

Peck is certainly one of their prized assets right now, and under contract until the summer of 2028, are under no immediate pressure to cash in, although, a decision on his future must be made sooner rather than later.

The England youth international's ceiling is extraordinarily high, and Sheffield United will be hoping to be the continued beneficiaries of his services for years to come, as time will tell exactly how this saga plays out.

Last month we got news that Wing Commander 3, the FMV space-combat sim with perhaps the highest concentration of Mark Hamill in any videogame, would be getting a remaster. Producer Jim Vessella is heading up the project, and revealed it during a presentation at the Portland Retro Gaming Expo.

What he did not reveal, it turns out, is that five other Wing Commander games—plus Road Rash—would be getting their own dolled-up re-releases, and that they'd all be dropping on Steam, GOG, and the EA App today as part of a larger bundle of 25 "EA Classics". No, I don't know why you'd buy them on the EA App either. It takes all sorts to make a world.

Here's the full list of games that are dropping. Anything labelled "Anniversary Edition" is an updated, remastered version of the original game. Anything not labelled that is simply a re-release.

If you're a GOG user, the only new things on that storefront will be the remasters—GOG's had all the other classic games for yonks now, but on Steam it's a veritable deluge of hearty, classic stuff. Personally, I'll just be glad to have a copy of Theme Hospital where all my other games are. And if someone fancies turning on Workshop support for it and integrating CorsixTH, they won't hear any objections from me.

On the remaster front, EA pinky promises it hasn't mucked the games up. "Each Anniversary Edition preserves the original look, feel and creative intent of the game, with quality-of-life updates to support modern systems, controls, improve performance and make the titles more approachable without compromising their authenticity."

You know what? I'm inclined to believe it. Vessella, who's more or less become the face of EA's old-game preservation and restoration efforts, also had a big hand in the work on the Command and Conquer Remastered Collection, and that was rather excellent even without access to the source footage of those games' FMV cutscenes, which seems to not be an issue here.

EA says the remasters are the fruit of "the discovery of a physical media vault containing thousands of Betacam, VHS, and D1 tapes alongside other media assets", while "the digitization process recovered the supposedly lost Master FMV sequences for these seven legendary titles, which were meticulously re-edited to restore the over 1,500 cinematic scenes across these games".

EA is bringing 25 classic PC games to Steam

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Electronic Arts is launching 25 classic games on Steam, including “Anniversary Editions” of the motorcycle racer Road Rash and six Wing Commander titles. These Anniversary Editions preserve “the original look, feel and creative intent of the game” but also have quality-of-life updates to support modern systems and controls and improve performance, according to EA.

The seven new editions were “inspired” by the discovery of a “physical media vault” with “thousands” of Betacam, VHS, and D1 tapes and “other media assets,” EA says. As part of the digitization process, EA was able to recover master FMV sequences and reedited them to restore more than 1,500 scenes from the games. The updated Wing Commander games also officially incorporate some community-made mods.

You’ll be able to get the Road Rash and Wing Commander Anniversary editions for $9.99, and the other classic games, including Ultima and Crusader titles, will be available for $4.99. You can grab them on Steam, GOG, and the EA App.

There's Demand to Remake Final Fantasy 6 from Within Square Enix, Says Series Boss

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

Could a remake of Final Fantasy 6 happen at some point? The idea's been floating around for a while, but we're now, apparently, at a point where Square Enix employees want to see it.

This is according to series veteran and current brand manager Yoshinori Kitase, who specifically notes that there's an internal demand for said remake while speaking to IGN.

The outlet asks Kitase for his thoughts on additional remake projects now that Final Fantasy 7 Remake's trilogy is coming to a close, and while he certainly doesn't commit to a definitive answer, his mention of Final Fantasy 6 is enough to raise eyebrows.

Kitase says that while he personally doesn't "have any particular feeling towards any kind of remake" at this moment in time, and that he doesn't believe he'll work on one "anytime soon", there's demand "inside the company".

"Inside the company and also among the Final Fantasy fans, there's been many people asking for a remake of Final Fantasy VI, so who knows?," he comments.

To me, this seems like the kind of seed that could sprout into something down the line. It's easy to forget that companies as big as Square Enix are seeing constant churn in terms of development teams and project leads; all it takes is for someone to gather support and pitch this potential remake at the right time.

Interestingly, though, it does sound like Kitase's priorities lie elsewhere. At the end of the interview, he says that he'd like to spearhead something with a focus on "battle action", but "without losing the characteristics of a Final Fantasy story".

After a 16-year long dev period, Faraway, a shooting star arcade game about siphoning the energy of other stars, is out now

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

Lord almighty, video games take a while to make, 'ey? Especially if you're just one person working on it by yourself. Such is the case with Faraway, out today, an arcade game that reminds me of the early days of PS3 indies "about a shooting star that creates constellations to stay alive," all made single-handedly by creator of cult-classic mobile game Eliss, Steph Thirion. Did I mention that Faraway took 16 years to make? Like I said, game development is a lengthy process.

Looking at Faraway, and knowing how long it took to produce, it doesn't surprise me to come from a dev who managed to release a mobile game in the early days of the platform, long before free-to-play got its chokehold on the industry. It also really does feel like it should be sitting amongst the likes of PixelJunk, this thing should be something I'm desperate to play on my PS3 while I wait for my controller to charge.

As mentioned, you are a shooting star, a "spark meant to shine and vanish in a blink." But still you persist, connecting to and wrapping yourself around starts to "harvest their energy," in turn lengthening your own life, and of course, in arcadey fashion, racking up a nice score. It looks like game design as poetry, everything you're looking at procedurally generated to bring freshness to every run. I can fully envision this as a "one more run" kind of arcade game, the best kind, especially in an age long past the need to shove in another of your hard earned coins.

Faraway conceptually charms me because it taps into a style of game we don't get to see much of any more. What I'm saying is, I'm just a teensy bit sick of how many roguelikes there are, but that doesn't mean I don't want a game I can just hop in and out of at my own whims. Sure, arcade games might not be in vogue, I just miss them a bit. In any case, Faraway is available to pick up on Steam right now. Seems like it'll be worth a punt!

Newcastle star Amar Dedic sparks six-car police swoop after being spotted with a BOW AND ARROWS in Northumberland beauty spot before weapon is seized

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

Published: 13:29 EDT, 5 November 2026 | Updated: 17:28 EDT, 5 November 2026

Newcastle United star Amar Dedic was at the centre of a major police operation after he was spotted carrying a bow and arrows at a Northumberland beauty spot – with at least SIX patrol cars involved in the search for the £30million defender.

The Bosnia international sparked alarm when a concerned member of the public reported seeing him and a friend walking with the weapon near a reservoir in the countryside outside Newcastle.

Police dispatched marked and unmarked vehicles to the area in Harlow Hill, with some understood to have been armed response units, before Dedic and his companion were finally intercepted around seven miles away in the exclusive Darras Hall estate.

Sources have described the dramatic swoop on Dedic's vehicle before officers searched the car and seized a bow and arrows, with the 24-year-old questioned about his activities.

In a bizarre twist, Newcastle goalkeeper Mark Gillespie and former defender Paul Dummett arrived at the scene while police were speaking to Dedic. It is understood the pair were in the area by chance and stopped to offer assistance.

After speaking to the two men, officers were satisfied there was no threat to the public, with police subsequently stating that the equipment was believed to have been intended for recreational use.

Amar Dedic played in Newcastle's 1-0 defeat by Everton on Monday evening

Dedic was spotted with a crossbow off Military Road, the 61-mile B6318 in Northumberland

However, enquiries remain ongoing into the incident, which happened on Wednesday afternoon. It is understood that Dedic and his friend had fired some arrows while in the countryside, although it remains unclear what they were aiming at. Hunting any animal or bird with a bow and arrow or crossbow is illegal in the UK.

Dedic joined Newcastle from Benfica during the summer and has impressed at right back despite the club's difficult start to the season.

Daily Mail Sport understands he will not face disciplinary action from Newcastle over the incident, although there is a feeling that the player was naive to believe he could walk around the countryside carrying such weaponry.

A Northumbria Police spokesperson said: 'Shortly after 1.55pm on Wednesday, we received a report of two men carrying a bow and arrows while walking along a track by the reservoir on Military Road in the Harlow Hill area.

'Officers attended and subsequently stopped a vehicle in the Darras Hall area and seized a bow and arrows. At this stage, it is understood they were for recreational use and there was no threat to the public. Enquiries are ongoing.'

Dedic is expected to be available for Saturday's Premier League trip to Fulham, where Newcastle will attempt to halt a run of four defeats in five matches across all competitions.

Swansea City receive significant boost as Vitor Matos issues injury update on key defender

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

Swansea City have hit a poor run of form after a phenomenal start to the season. The Swans were top of the pile after 12 games, but a run of three defeats in four games has seen them drop to 4th, with a midweek defeat to Portsmouth the latest in that run.

During that Pompey clash, summer signing Stephen Welsh went off with a knee problem that appeared serious. However, as they head to Birmingham City this weekend, Vitor Matos has revealed that the injury is not as bad as first thought and he could be in contention to face Chris Davies’ side, as reported by Wales Online.

Matos said: “We will still assess if he will be available for Saturday but it’s not a bad injury. It’s just a knock that is really really painful.”

Welsh has made a great impression since making the switch from Celtic in the summer. Eom Jisung and Moussa Yeo are back available for the Swans this weekend as well, while Ben Cabango is also fit despite a recent knock.

Welsh has quickly established himself as one of Swansea City’s most important players since arriving on a free transfer from Celtic in summer 2026. The 26-year-old signed a two-year contract, with the option of a further year, becoming Vitor Matos’ first summer recruit.

Cameron Burgess’ long-term Achilles injury opened the door for Welsh, who has since made the centre-back position his own. He has started 13 of Swansea’s 14 Championship matches, primarily alongside Ben Cabango, and has impressed with his composure, defensive work and ability to play out from the back.

His passing accuracy, progressive distribution and contribution to Swansea’s defensive organisation have made him an ideal fit for Matos’ possession-based approach. His performances have also earned him his first senior Scotland caps.

With Swansea challenging near the top of the table, Welsh has become a key figure. Securing an experienced defender of his quality on a free transfer is proving to be excellent summer business.

Swansea are short of centre-back options without Welsh, although their defensive depth remains manageable in the short term.

Cabango is the obvious leader, while Jenson Seelt offers the most natural replacement after already featuring under Matos. The Sunderland loanee would be the likely partner for Cabango if Welsh is unavailable.

Filip Lissah provides further cover, having demonstrated his versatility by operating centrally, but regularly relying on him could weaken the right-back position. Cameron Burgess remains sidelined with a long-term Achilles injury, leaving Swansea more exposed if Welsh faces an extended absence.
